欧州大規模デモが問う財政再建の道 
金融市場が動揺するリスクは当面残存する見通し

○ このところ欧州ではインフレに伴う生活苦や公共サービスの劣化等を背景に各地で大規模抗議活
動が発生している 
○ 大規模デモは緊縮を伴う財政健全化を難しくする要因となる。財政懸念や政情不安は国債利回り
の上昇やユーロ安を招いており、経済への悪影響も懸念される 
○ 来年にかけて、欧州では複数の重要な国政選挙が予定される中、政治・財政を巡る不安が欧州金
融市場を動揺させるリスクは当面残存するだろう

１．欧州で広がる大規模抗議活動 
このところ、欧州では大規模デモが多発している（図表1）。フランスでは、9月以降、教員不足や
学校施設の不備など、教育環境の改善を求める高校生の抗議活動が続いていた。こうした中で、10月
半ばには、物価高への対策や公共サービスの改善を求める市民も加わり、抗議活動は全国規模に拡大
した。 
大規模デモが発生しているのはフランスだけではない。フランスのデモに一部触発される形でベル
図表 1 9 月以降に欧州で発生した主な抗議活動 図表2 EU：生活苦の割合（左）と 
自国が抱える課題（右）

### Page 2

2

ギーやイタリア等でも抗議活動が発生している。また、スペインでは、高齢女性が家賃を支払えずに
強制退去させられたことをきっかけに、住宅不足や住宅価格・家賃高騰等に対する不満が爆発する形
で大規模な抗議活動が発生し、これが遠因となりサンチェス首相は議会の解散に追い込まれた。 
各地で発生する大規模抗議活動の背景には、近年のインフレによる生活苦やこれを受けた政府への
不満がある1。EUにおいて、生活苦を訴える人の割合は低中所得層を中心に2022年以降高まっている
ほか、 自国が直面する課題としてインフレや経済状況を挙げる人の割合も高い （図表2）。これらの要
因が、自国政府に対する信頼感の低迷につながっている（図表3）。 
本稿では、生活改善を求めて各国で広がる抗議活動が経済や財政、金融市場に与える影響について
考察する。 
２．大規模抗議活動は財政健全化を阻む要因に 
一連の抗議活動は、2018年にフランスで発生した黄色いベスト運動を想起させる2。 当時の参加者は、
自動車に常備が義務付けられている黄色い安全ベストを着用し、燃料税の増税や生活費高騰に抗議し
た。購買力の低下や政府への不満が背景にある点は当時と共通している。異なるのは、政府の追加的
な財政支出の余地が、当時と比べてさらに狭まっていることである。 
欧州債務危機が起きた2010年代前半以降、順調に進捗してきた政府債務の削減は、2020年代に入る
と足踏みしはじめた。パンデミック時の大規模給付やウクライナ戦争後のエネルギー高対策など、危
機対応の財政出動が頻発したためだ。黄色いベスト運動が発生した2018年と比べて政府債務が増加し
たEU加盟国は、27カ国中18カ国にのぼる（図表4）。 
加えて、足元では防衛費増額や社会保障費・利払い負担の拡大など、構造的な財政支出圧力も高ま
っている。 各国の次年度予算案を踏まえると、 財政赤字は当面、高止まりする公算が大きい （図表5） 。
安全保障環境の変化やエネルギー価格の高騰を踏まえ、EUは一定の防衛費・エネルギー関連の追加支
出をEU財政ルールの対象外とする柔軟化措置を設けている。 しかし、 当該措置を適用しているドイツ
図表3 EU市民の自国政府への信頼度 図表4 EU加盟国の一般政府債務残高の変化 
（2018年～2025年）

### Page 3

3

やベルギーでは、特例対象の追加支出を除いてもなお、財政赤字をGDP比3％に抑制するEUの基準を
超過する見込みである。それでもベルギーでは、連立与党間の予算協議が決裂し、首相が辞意を表明
する事態に陥った。 
フランスを起点に広がった抗議活動は、 緊縮を伴う財政健全化の難しさを浮き彫りにしている。2018
年の黄色いベスト運動では、マクロン政権が燃料税増税の撤回や所得支援策の拡充に踏み切ったこと
で、抗議活動が漸く沈静化に向かった。今回は、11月に新たなデモやストライキが計画されるなど、
いまだ抗議活動が収束する見通しは立っていない。各国政府は事態の長期化を回避するため、家計支
援や公共サービス拡充などの対応を迫られる可能性があり、財政拡張圧力が一段と強まることが懸念
される。 
３．政治・財政を巡る不安が欧州金融市場を動揺させるリスクは当面残存 
金融市場は、欧州主要国の財政状況に対する警戒を強めている。 
フランスをはじめとする高債務国では、政治や財政を巡る混乱を嫌気して、10年債利回りが上昇し
ている（図表6）。2010年代の欧州債務危機を受けてECB（欧州中央銀行）は、条件付きで対象国の国
債を無制限に購入可能なOMT（Outright Monetary Transactions）や経済の基礎的条件では正当化されな
い国債市場の混乱が金融政策の波及を阻害する場合に発動可能なTPI （Transmission Protection 
Instrument）などの市場安定化措置3を整備してきた。当時よりセーフティーネットが整備されている
こともあり、足元の国債利回り上昇が欧州債務危機時のような金融危機につながる可能性は現時点で
は低い。 
とはいえ、金利の上昇は景気下押しや、さらなる財政悪化の要因となる。金利上昇幅が大きいフラ
ンスを例に足元の利回り上昇幅を踏まえて試算すると、設備投資・住宅投資が1年後に3％程度減少す
るとの結果が得られる （図表7） 。金利上昇やそれに伴う景気減速は、利払い費の増加や税収の減少を
図表5 主要国の財政収支実績と見通し 図表6 ユーロ圏の高債務国と 
ドイツの10年債利回り

きなかった国。数値は成立前の政府予算案ベース。

### Page 4

4

通じて、財政をさらに悪化させる要因にもなる。 
各国の政情不安や財政への懸念は、ECBの金融政策運営をも左右する可能性がある。足元では、ユ
ーロが主要通貨に対して下落している （図表8） 。 ユーロ安は、 輸入物価の上昇を通じてインフレ率を
押し上げる要因となる。足元のユーロ下落幅を前提に、時系列モデルを用いてユーロ圏のインフレ率
への影響を試算すると、総合インフレ率は0.3％Pt程度、コアインフレ率は0.1％Pt程度押し上げられる
との結果が得られた（図表9）。 ユーロ圏では、イラン情勢の緊迫化に伴うエネルギー価格の高騰を背
景に、直近でもインフレ率が加速している。先行きユーロ安圧力が強い状況が続けば、インフレ率に
一段の上昇圧力がかかる可能性がある。しかし、ECBはインフレ圧力が一段と強まっても、状況によ
っては追加利上げに踏み切りにくくなることが想定される。利上げがさらなる長期金利の上昇、国債
市場の不安定化を招くおそれがあるためだ。このように政情不安や財政懸念は、ECBの金融政策運営
を困難にする要因にもなりかねない。 
各国の財政や政治情勢に対する金融市場の懸念は当面残存する可能性が高い。欧州では、今年11月
にスペイン議会選挙、来年にはフランス大統領選挙やイタリア議会選挙など、重要な国政選挙が相次
いで予定されている。大規模デモの発生に加えて選挙も控える中、各国政府は国民に負担を求める緊
縮的な財政政策を例年以上に打ち出しにくくなっている。また、各国の選挙では極右政党やポピュリ
スト政党の勢力拡大が予想され、選挙後の政治はより一層不安定化するリスクがある。このほか、コ
ロナ禍からの経済復興を目的に設立されたEU復興基金の受け取りが終了することも、 来年以降の財政
懸念を強める要因になるだろう。同基金の配分が手厚かった南欧や中東欧諸国は、自国の財政負担を
抑えながら公共投資などを通じて経済成長を支えてきたが、来年以降はこうした効果が徐々に剥落す
ることになる。

### Page 5

5

以上のように、2020年代に入ってから、コロナ禍におけるサプライチェーン混乱、ロシアのウクラ
イナ侵攻やイラン情勢の緊迫によるエネルギー高が立て続けに起きてインフレが継続する中、欧州各
国で有権者の生活コスト上昇に対する不満が強まっている。一方、これらの緊急事態に対応するため
財政出動を繰り返してきたことに加え、高齢化に伴う社会保障費の増加、地政学リスクの高まりを背
景とした防衛費増強の必要性、利払い費の増大に直面しており、各国の財政余力は著しく低下してい
る。来年にかけて予定されている主要国の選挙では、極右・ポピュリスト政党の勢力拡大が予想され
ており、政治の混乱に拍車がかかる懸念もある。欧州の政治情勢や財政状況に対する不安が来年にか
けて沈静化するとは考えにくく、国債市場や為替市場が動揺するリスクに引き続き留意する必要があ
ろう。

おかつ実質賃金の伸び率が当該国の長期平均を下回る場合、反政府デモの発生件数が約 7.9％増加すると推計している

市場焦點
美國
• 10月S&P Global綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖較前月回落，但仍處高檔區間；服務業動
能略為放緩，製造業PMI則升至56.0，創近4年半新高。新訂單增速加快 ，顯示企業需求、投
資及庫存回補動能仍具韌性 ，然而企業售價與投入成本同步上升 ，反映需求改善 、供應鏈延
遲及成本轉嫁效應持續存在 ，通膨降溫進程仍具不確定性 。整體而言 ，市場關注焦點轉向通
膨與利率走勢，高利率環境恐維持較長時間，進而推高股債資產評價波動。
中國
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季 GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期 ，在出口與科技製造業帶動下 ，
短期成長可望維持穩定 ，但若內需未能有效改善 ，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散 ，建議聚焦在
半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈 ，及高端製造相關產業 ，至於消費 、房地產及傳統內需類股 ，

仍需觀察後續刺激政策與消費信心回升情況。
法國
政局動盪
• 法國金融市場因各地抗議活動持續擴大 ，面臨股債雙跌的局面。市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支
出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮 。法國10年期公債殖利率一度突破 5%，法德利差亦擴
大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標仍處於歷史相對低檔 ，遠低於歐
元區主權債務危機期間，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務危機的風險仍相對有限。

美國PMI維持強勁擴張，經濟成長續航但通膨壓力再升溫
• 企業需求強勁支撐美國景氣維持高檔擴張 ：美國10月綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖略低於前月，但仍處於近五年來
的高位區間，顯示企業活動持續高速擴張；其中服務業 PMI為57.9、製造業PMI升至56.0並創53個月新高，新訂單增速
更創逾4年半最佳表現，反映內需穩健、出口需求改善與企業補庫存效應同步發酵，美國第四季經濟動能仍具韌性。
• 成本上升與供應瓶頸推升通膨風險： 企業售價漲幅創2022年8月以來最高，能源、運輸、金屬原料及薪資成本同步上揚 ，
加上供應鏈交期延長與訂單積壓擴大，使企業得以持續轉嫁成本，暗示未來數月消費者通膨可能再度偏離聯準會目標區
間，市場對利率維持較高水準的預期恐因此升溫。
• 後市觀察：景氣韌性有利獲利成長，但通膨可能推升殖利率、壓抑高評價資產。投資配置建議優先布局美國大型科技股 、

美國金融股及工業資本支出受惠族群，可透過NASDAQ 100 ETF、S&P 500 ETF及美國銀行股ETF參與企業獲利成長；
固定收益則以短天期投資級債為主，以降低利率波動風險。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.24

海外資金流入美國市場，支撐美國資產吸引力
• 海外資金持續流入美國：美國財政部8月TIC資料顯示，
整體資金淨流入 1,998億美元，其中民間部門淨流入
1,785億美元，為主要資金來源；外資淨買進美國長
期證券3,007億美元，遠高於美國投資人加碼海外資
產216億美元，反映全球資金在經濟成長與流動性考
量下，對美國金融市場仍維持高度的配置需求。
• 全球資金偏好美國資產： 外資 8月淨買入美國股票
1,319億美元，高於美國公債與公司債 ，顯示海外投
資人仍看好美國企業獲利與經濟基本面 ，雖然短期國
庫券持有量下降，部分跨境美元資金亦出現波動，但
以10月近況來看長天期公債標售仍吸引境外央行 、
保險資金及大型資產管理機構參與，反映高殖利率水
準對長期資金仍具吸引力。在經濟成長維持韌性與企

業獲利支撐下，美國資產仍為全球資金配置的重要核
心市場。
• 市場看法：海外資金流入有助反映美國資產吸引力，
惟後續仍須觀察通膨 、Fed政策及企業獲利表現 。投
資布局建議可以美國投資等級債、美國中短天期公債
及大型AI基礎建設受惠族群為核心配置 ，兼顧收益、
防禦及長期成長潛力。

歷史數據顯示全球股市突破歷史新高後，後續上漲機率高
• 很多人直覺以為見高就是頂，但過去20年的統計資料顯示，全球股市在創下新高後，多數時間選擇繼續向上走。
• 勝率顯著偏高： 全球股市 (MSCI全球指數 )創新高後持有一年 ，獲得正報酬的機率高達 62%，三個月後的正報酬機率更達
71%。美股與台股表現最亮眼，標普500指數創高後一年上漲機率為76%，那斯達克為79%；而台灣股市表現更為驚人，創
高後三個月內上漲的機率高達82%。
• 下檔拉回風險有限：在創高後短期(一個月內)修正幅度其實相當溫和 ，全球多數主要市場的回徹幅度中位數僅 1%～2%左右。
• 突破前高優於連續創新高： 若市場在歷經至少一個月以上的整理後突破前高 ，其未來一年的平均報酬率高達 8.4%，遠高於

連續每天創新高的3.9%。這代表市場經適度整理後的突破，具備更健康的上漲動能。
創新高後的平均遠期報酬率
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.22

顧
市場寬度收窄，由少數巨頭獨撐
• 當前的市場特徵由少數巨頭帶頭衝： 科技股權重較高的指數(如那斯達克、台股、韓股)，長期以來都有少數權重股拉動指數
的現象。自2024年以來，全球13個主要市場中有10個市場在創新高時，參與上漲的股票比例明顯下降 。已開發市場創新高
時高於200日均線的股票比例從過去 75.5%降至65.1%，新興市場更降至 61.3%。這意味著指數看似風光 ，但多數個股並未
同步創高，當漲勢過度集中於少數大型科技巨頭時，整體市場的抗風險能力會相對減弱。
• 小結與投資建議：歷史經驗顯示，創新高本身是強勢市場的特徵，後續繼續走揚的機率遠大於立刻崩盤。然而，鑑於目前市
場寬度收窄、權重高度集中於科技股的現狀 ，盲目追高個股的風險已顯著增加 。投資建議可以利用ETF掌握大盤趨勢，由於

指數上漲主要由龍頭股驅動 ，透過市值型ETF(如追蹤標普500ETF)能直接享受龍頭巨頭帶來的指數創高紅利 ，避免選錯個股
而賺了指數、賠了價差。此外，提防短期股市回檔，可運用定期定額分批布局或等待大盤適度拉回整理再行加碼，降低追高
風險。
創新高時不同水準的市場寬度佔比

法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓
• 法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓：法國各地抗議活動持續擴大，最初由學生發起，隨後因生活成本高漲而引發其
他群體響應。在財政空間有限、俄烏戰爭與美伊衝突推升能源價格的背景下，政府難以有效緩解民生成本壓力，法國金融市
場因此面臨股債雙跌的局面。
• 公債殖利率攀升，但系統性風險仍相對可控：市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮。法國10
年期公債殖利率一度突破5%，法德利差亦擴大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標（CISS）仍處於
歷史相對低檔，遠低於歐元區主權債務危機期間，顯示金融市場尚未出現廣泛的系統性壓力，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務
危機的風險仍相對有限。

日本央行本週料維持利率不變
日本央行利率走勢預估
資料來源： Bloomberg，2026.10.15 資料來源： tradingeconomics，2026.10.21
日本貿易收支
• 日本貿易逆差擴大，進口增幅高於出口：日本2026年9月貿易逆差擴大至5,366億日圓，較去年同期的2,777億日圓增加93%，
高於市場預期的1,810億日圓，連續第五個月呈現貿易赤字。出口年增16.7%至10.97兆日圓，受惠於半導體相關電子零件、
製造設備及汽車出口成長；惟進口年增18.9%至11.51兆日圓，增幅高於出口，主要受到原油、半導體電子零件及液化天然
氣進口增加帶動。其中，原油進口年增51.1%，部分反映日本在荷姆茲海峽局勢緊張下，積極分散能源採購來源。
• 日本央行本週料維持利率不變：日本央行9月將政策利率調升25個基點至1.25%，創31年來新高。考量前幾次升息對經濟的

傳導效果仍待觀察，加上貿易逆差擴大反映進口成本壓力，多數分析師預期日本央行本週將暫停升息，以評估貨幣緊縮對經
濟與通膨的影響。

顧
中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期：顯示中國經濟在第二季落底後呈現溫和回升 ，從季度表現來看，
一、二、三季增速分別為5.0%、4.3%及4.5%，反映政策支持與外需復甦開始發揮效果 ，全年達成官方4.5%成長目標的機率
明顯提升，第三季經濟改善的主要動能來自出口 ，受惠於全球企業持續擴建 AI基礎設施，及科技產品需求強勁 ，中國高科技
產品出口表現亮眼 ，尤其是積體電路 、伺服器、電腦零組件，及儲存設備等產品增幅顯著 ，高技術製造業維持雙位數成長 。
同時，中國對美國及歐盟出口維持強勁 ，推升貿易順差 走升，為支撐經濟的重要力量 。然而，內需疲弱仍是當前經濟最大隱
憂，房地產調整尚未結束，居民消費與企業投資意願保守，使經濟復甦結構呈現「外熱內冷」的特徵。

• 展望後市：在出口與科技製造業帶動下 ，短期成長可望維持穩定 ，但若內需未能有效改善 ，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散 ，建
議聚焦在半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈 ，及高端製造相關產業 ，至於消費、房地產及傳統內需類股 ，仍需觀察後續刺激政
策與消費信心回升情況。
資料來源：開源證券，2026.10.22

彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高
• 彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高：彭博產業研究認為，中國AI產業未來將由大型科技平台主導 ，其中騰
訊、字節跳動與阿里巴巴憑藉龐大用戶基礎 、數據資源、資金實力及完整生態系統 ，在AI競賽中具備明顯優勢 ，這些企業可將
AI廣泛應用於電商、社群、遊戲、金融與雲端業務，提升營運效率並強化競爭門檻，例如：阿里巴巴利用 AI 驅動電商平台上的
個人化產品推薦，而騰訊則運用 AI 優化短影音廣告並強化微信功能。騰訊自 2026 年 7 月起開始將混元 AI 模型整合至其電子
遊戲中，使遊戲角色及助理能更準確地回應玩家 ，然而，AI模型訓練與基礎建設投入龐大 ，獲利能力短期仍受壓抑 ，且須面對
監管、資料隱私及晶片供應等挑戰 。此外，智譜、月之暗面、MiniMax及商湯等AI新創雖具技術創新能力，但缺乏規模效益、

穩定現金流及長期資本支持，未來獲利挑戰較大。
• 投資建議：看好中國AI長期發展者，可優先關注具資金 、算力與應用場景優勢的大型平台企業 ，受惠程度可望高於多數 AI新創
公司。
資料來源：彭博，2026.10.21

台灣9月外銷訂單再創新高，台股第四季有望先蹲後跳
• 台灣9月外銷訂單再創新高： 9月外銷訂單1,142.1億美元，連兩個月破千億美元 ，再創單月新高；年增率高達 62.4%，連續
20個月呈雙位數正成長 。經濟部表示，AI強勁需求超乎預期 ，預估今年第四季接單還會優於第三季 。展望10月表現，外銷
訂單將持續亮麗，估落在1,125億至1,159億美元，有望連三個月破千億美元。
• 台股第四季有望先蹲後跳： 10月初以來，台股在盤中觸及歷史高點 49,968點後面臨一波修正 ，主要反映在新一輪的財報公
布前，市場觀望AI投資是否存在泡沫化風險 。短期來看，台股在季線附近出現支撐 ，不過在11月初的美國期中選舉落幕前 ，
台股維持區間震盪的可能性較高；中期來看，美國期中選後往往因政治不確定性下降，歷史統計股市上漲機率高，加上台灣

年底集團作帳及選舉行情，正向看待年底台股表現。
資料來源：經濟部統計處，兆豐銀行整理，2026.10.20。

前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。

1 群益投顧 
國巨 (2327 TT) 整體 3Q26 財報表現優異，其中營收為 495.3 億元 (QoQ +11.4% 、 YoY 
+49.7%) ，主因受惠 AI 帶 動 相 關 被 動 元 件 增 溫 ， 其 中AI 占比較 2Q26 的 16% 提升至
近 20% ； EPS 5.83 元，優於 預期 。 然 展 望 4Q26 ，受 PC 市 場 疲 軟 及 消 費 性 電 子 需 求
放緩抵銷預期 AI 於 4Q 帶 來 的 強 勁 增 長，國 巨 4Q26 營收將從預估季增雙位數下修至
微 幅 季 增，不 過 因 VR 將於 2027 年 正 式 放 量，被 動 產 能 供 需 將 會 更 緊，故 維 持 整 體 被
動 元 件 漲 價 趨 勢 向 上 ， 看 法 為 短 空 長 多 。

研 究 員 ： 葉 芷 瑄   
vanessa.yeh@cim.capital.com.tw

國巨 3Q26 法 說 會 重 點：  
國巨 (2327 TT) 於昨 (29) 舉辦 3Q26 法 說 會 ， 整 體 3Q26 財 報 表 現 優 異 ， 其 中
營收為 495.3 億元 (QoQ +11.4% 、 YoY +49.7%) ， 主 因 受 惠 AI 帶 動 相 關 被 動
元 件 增 溫 ， 其 中 AI 占比較 2Q26 的 16% 提 升 至 近 20% ； 毛 利 率 因 產 品 組 合
轉佳至 41.0%(QoQ +2.5ppts 、 YoY +4.8ppts) ； OPM 29.5%(QoQ +3.1ppts 、
YoY +6.6ppts) ； 歸 屬 母 公 司 稅 後 淨 利 為 119.8 億元 (QoQ +27.2% 、 YoY 
+88.5%) ；EPS 5.83 元 ， 優 於 群 益 預 估 的 5.15 元 。 然 展 望 4Q26 ，受 PC 市場
疲 軟 及 消 費 性 電 子 需 求 放 緩 抵 銷 預 期 AI 於 4Q 帶 來 的 強 勁 增 長 ，BB ratio
略 較 前 季 呈 現 微 幅 下 滑，國 巨 4Q26 營 收 將 從 預 估 季 增 雙 位 數 下 修 至 微 幅 季
增 ， 不 過 因 VR 將於 2027 年 正 式 放 量 ， 被 動 產 能 供 需 將 會 更 緊 ， 故 維 持 整
體 被 動 元 件 漲 價 趨 勢 向 上 ， 看 法 為 短 空 長 多 。

2 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We update our view on Advantech after 3Q26 results 
(released on 4 November).

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results beat. Advantech’s 3Q26 earnings of 
TWD6.2bn (EPS: TWD7.15) beat our estimate and BBG consensus by 
26% and 28%, respectively, driven by record-high OPM (20.9%) and better-
than-expected non-ops (gains from its Japan plant’s asset disposal). 
However, GM fell 1pp QoQ to 36.7%, mainly due to raw-material cost 
inflation. Management said CPU, memory and other component costs rose 
to 70% (vs. 30% previously). Although the company could pass on higher 
costs, it couldn’t maintain its blended GM on an incremental sales.

Business update. Looking ahead, Advantech remains positive on its 4Q26 
and 2027 outlook, supported by a c.USD2.5bn order backlog and a 3Q26 
B/B ratio of 1.64 (vs. 1.6 in October). Management said cost-inflation-driven 
demand pull-ins were limited in 9M26, but order visibility has extended as 
clients seek greater allocations. Advantech sees AI infrastructure, 
semiconductors, factory automation and medical equipment as key growth 
drivers, with shipments in these applications expected to grow c.15% YoY 
in 2026E and into 2027E, based on our forecasts. Edge AI remains its 
fastest-growing application: its revenue contribution rose to 24.5% in 9M26, 
with c.109% YoY growth, vs. 22.5% in 1H26 and the company’s c.30% 
target for 2026. Growth has been driven mainly by higher ASPs, but we 
expect unit growth to become the primary driver in 2027E as ASPs lap the 
increase in 2026 already. In the near term, while management guides its 
revenue would decrease by single-digit % QoQ due to fewer working days 
over Christmas holiday period in 4Q26, but we believe this is too 
conservative. We expect 4Q26E revenue to be broadly flat QoQ, as a solid 
backlog is on hand. We also expect gross margin to remain similar to 3Q26 
amid ongoing cost inflation. Management reported ASP increases of 10-
20% for CPUs and 15-20% for PCBs, while SSD and memory prices have 
been relatively stable recently. We expect shortages to ease gradually, 
particularly for memory, but to persist for CPUs and CCLs.

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 7-22% to reflect 
Advantech’s 3Q26 results. We reaffirm our Outperform (2) call and raise 
our 12M TP to TWD750 (from TWD700), based on a lower PER of 26x 
(prior: 30x; slightly higher than the mid-point of its past-3-year range of 20-
37x), as we are closer to the end of the up-cycle, and applied to our 1-year 
forward EPS. Key downside risk: slower-than-expected demand recovery.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 10-17% above the consensus, 
likely due to our more bullish outlook on Advantech’s revenue.

4 November 2026

3Q26 results: positive tone towards 2027

➢ Up-cycle will last into 2027, with limited early pull-in shipments in 2026 
➢ Key growth drivers in 2026 include ASP hike and fast Edge AI growth 
➢ Reaffirming our Outperform (2) rating; raising 12-month TP to TWD750

Company profile 
Founded in 1981 and listed in 1999, Advantech is the largest industrial PC (IPC) maker worldwide in 
terms of embedded board/system sales, followed by the company’s main competitors, Germany-based 
Kontron and US-based Radisys. The company mainly provides embedded computing boards, chassis, 
industrial computers, applied computers and design-in services. The businesses are categorised into 4 
segments: Embedded design-in, Industrial IoT, Service IoT, and Global services.

GUC
Company description
Founded in 1998, Global Unichip (GUC) specializes in providing IC design
services, particularly in ASIC service. As a subsidiary of TSMC, which holds
34.8% of GUC, it offers a wide range of design services including spec-in and
SoC integration, physical implementation, 2.5D/3D packaging technologies,
mass production services, and advanced technologies such as high
bandwidth memory (HBM) and chip to chip interconnect IP .  With its

diversified global presence – Taiwan, North America, China, Europe, South
Korea, and Japan – the company offers services including mass production,
NRE, customer wafer verification projects, and IP licensing. Mass production
services provide customers with complete service, from back-end design
completion to wafer manufacturing, testing and management.
Investment strategy
We rate GUC stock as Buy/High Risk. We are seeing strong growth from its
CPU projects with two leading CSP customers. We also see strong order

potential from its EV clients. Long-term, we still believe back-end design
capability and 3DIC system integration will be critical to AI accelerators,
which would give better opportunities for TSMC 3DFabric partners with GUC
also being a beneficiary, in our view.
Valuation
Our target price for GUC of NT$8,000 (rounded) is based on 37x PER to the
average of our 2027 /28E EPS, slightly above its historical 3-year PER average
which we believe is justified by its strong earnings outlook supported by solid

demand from its AI projects with two US leading CSPs and growth upside
from its US EV clients. Our target price equates to 2027 /28E PB of 30x/14x.
Risks
Our quant risk rating system rates the GUC stock High Risk for its share-price
volatility. Downside risks that could mean the stock trades below our target
price include: 1) slower projects demand from US CSP customers, 2) lower-
than-expected NRE demand from less AI ASIC projects, and 3) delayed or less

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

Hon Hai (2317.TW): Oct in line: revenues +46% YoY; New AI servers, CPO 
switch and Foldable phones to drive 4Q26; Buy (on CL)
6 November 2026 | 1:57PM HKT
Hon Hai’s Oct revenues were +46% YoY/ 13% MoM to NT$1.3tn, or 4% higher than 
our estimates, reflecting strong AI servers ramp up and new smartphone models in 
contribution. From a MoM perspective, Consumer electronics was the strongest, 
followed by Computing, Components, and Cloud and Networking. Consumer 
electronics, Computing and Components segments are positive MoM, while Cloud 
and Networking saw slight decline MoM, due to the shift by consignment model, 
tight supply, and early-stage production ramps of new models, despite the fact that 
AI-related demand remained strong. The strong MoM of the other 3 segments 
reaffirms our positive view on the company’s leading market position across: (1) 
Smartphone: serving global leading brands, which suffer less under high memory 
costs, with new model launches and form factor changes (report link), driving the 
shipment and ASP increase, (2) AI servers: serving global leading CSP customers, 
and early in the delivery of the latest AI server racks, driving the shipment and ASP 
increase. The company is also the major supplier of CPO switch, supporting 4Q26 
growth. From a YoY perspective, Cloud and Networking was the strongest, followed 
by Consumer electronics, Components and Computing, and all segments were 
positive YoY. We expect to see both YoY and QoQ growth throughout 4Q26, driven 
by new AI server racks, new smartphone models, and CPO switches.

3-month revenues preview: We expect company’s 4Q26E revenues to be +40% 
YoY/ +21% QoQ to NT$3.7 trillion. 3Q26 delivered strong revenues YoY growth of 
47%, or 5% / 7% ahead of our / Bloomberg consensus, and we expect to see both 
YoY and QoQ growth throughout 4Q26, driven by new AI server racks, new

remain on track to maintain solid growth momentum through 4Q26, while ICT products

EV business in Oct 2026: Hon Hai’s EV subsidiary Foxtron (2258.TW, Not Covered)

NT$114mn). In Oct 2026, Foxtron ranked No.2 with cumulative YTD sales volume 
reaching 3,986 units (news).

Earnings revision: We factor in Hon Hai Oct revenues and revised down 2026E net 
income by 5%, mainly on lower revenues due to weak consumer electronics demand. We 
remain positive on AI server racks ramp up, Hon Hai’s leading market position in terms of 
scale, technology, and execution, and rising foldable phones to drive growth ahead. We 
keep our 2027-28E estimates largely unchanged.

### Page 3

Valuation: Our 12m TP for Hon Hai is unchanged at NT$400 and we continue to derive it 
through near-term P/E. Our target 2027E P/E multiple of 17.9x (unchanged) is derived 
from peers’ average ratio of trading P/E to forward year NI YoY growth and OPM (P/E 
divided by the sum of NI YoY and OPM). Our target P/E of 17.9x is in the range of the 
company’s historical forward P/E since 2010, reflecting our positive view on Hon Hai’s 
business expansion from the competitive consumer electronics market to AI servers and 
EVs outsourcing, which should drive profit expansion with diversifying business 
opportunities for Hon Hai. Maintain Buy (on AP AC CL).

Price Target Risks and Methodology – Hon Hai 
Valuation methodology: We are Buy rated on Hon Hai. Our 12-month target price of 
NT$400 is based on an 17.9x 2027E P/E multiple, which is set in line with peers’ PEG&M 
ratio (i.e., P/E vs. forward-year earnings growth and OPM). 
Key downside risks: (1) slower-than-expected ramp-up of the AI server business; (2) 
weaker-than-expected EV total solution performance across EV assembly, design,

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

Delta Electronics Inc.  | Asia Pacific
October  Sales +14% MoM/
+30% YoY
In this report, we focus on Delta's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• October sales came in at NT$74,666mn (+14% MoM/+30% YoY), driven by
the increasing shipment of power supply units, HVDC power racks, and liquid
cooling products.
• QTD revenue reached 35% of MSe at NT$215,881mn (+10% QoQ/+34% YoY),
and 34% of consensus at NT$219,806mn (+12% QoQ/+36% YoY).

Our view:
• We think 4Q26 revenue is tracking slightly ahead of market expectations.
• We think the slightly better Oct monthly sales should help on near-term
sentiment, though more meaningful upside to market expectations on
earnings growth might  depend on the penetration of HVDC power racks into
the coming years.
• We continue to see Delta as a key beneficiary of the increasing power
consumption of AI clusters through its comprehensive power/cooling

offerings. Stay OW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Delta Electronics Inc. (2308.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 30x based on average 2027-28e EPS.                          We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 59% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.5, versus
the stock's ~23x P/E and 23% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 1.0.
Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected global economic growth

n Falling raw material prices
n Margin improvement
Risks to Downside
n Global economic slowdown
n Ongoing component supply tightness
n Rising raw material prices
n Margin contraction as a result of higher operating expenses or faster ASP erosion

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

研 究  報 告                
啟碁(6285.TT) 
產業：電子–通信網路 
無法及時反映原物料漲價，3Q26 毛利率承壓 
 投資評價與建議 
受惠Wi-Fi 7與5G FWA滲透率提升且低軌衛星UT與800G交換器逐步放量，預
估2026/2027 年稅後EPS為13.25/14.23元，過往啟碁forward PE區間落在
15~20倍 ， 考量原物料成本上漲壓抑毛利率 ， 基於2027年獲利 ， 給予15倍PER 。 
 重點評論分析 
無法及時反映原物料漲價，3Q26毛利率承壓，4Q26持續受到影響：3Q26營
收季增23.5%至487億元，係因北美客戶拉貨需求強勁，毛利率季減0.77ppts
至12.2% ， 係因3Q26調整售價僅單純反映原物料漲價成本 ， 並非等比例調整售
價 ， 導致毛利率承壓 ， 目前公司目標將毛利率維持在12%以上之水準 ， 稅後EPS
為4.35元 。 預估4Q26營收季增3.1%至501.8億元 ， 由於目前原物料漲價品項多
且公司與客戶間多有預定合約價格，故無法及時反映漲價，若能漲價亦僅轉嫁
成本，獲利空間遭壓縮，故下修毛利率至12.1%，稅後EPS為3.15元。 
網通規格升級為主要獲利引擎：展望2026~2027年，主要營收動能來自Wi-Fi 
7與5G FWA網通規格升級以及美系低軌衛星客戶終端裝置部署量持續提升。
啟碁目前交換器業務主要透過Tier 1 Switch供應商夥伴間接出貨予CSP客戶，
下世代1.6T規格以及OCS預於2027年送樣 ，目標2028年開始貢獻營收。 4Q26
為是否於美國設廠之關鍵期，公司將視FCC、USMCA與關稅政策等以及客戶
需求，針對涉及資安和國安相關列管之產品線遷至美國生產。 
 投資風險 
原物料漲價與缺料、低軌衛星產品出現競爭者。

### Page 2

2

| 營運概況  
無法及時反映原物料漲價，3Q26毛利率承壓，4Q26持續受到影響：3Q26營收季增23.5%至487億元，主要受惠北美洲
Service Provider與Enterprise客戶拉貨需求強勁，毛利率季減0.77ppts至12.2%，係因3Q26調 整售價僅單純反映原物
料漲價成本，並非等比例調整售價，導致毛利率承壓，目前公司目標將毛利率維持在12%以上之水準，稅後EPS為4.35
元。隨Enterprise、Service Provider與Automotive三大事業群持續開拓新產品、專案與客戶，預估4Q26營收季增3.1%
至501.8億元，由於目前原物料漲價品項多，從Memory擴及PCB板、MLCC、成熟製程IC等零組件，且公司與客戶間多
有預定合約價格，故無法及時反映漲價，若能漲價亦僅轉嫁成本，獲利空間遭壓縮，故下修毛利率至12.1%，公司表示
4Q26至2027全年將持續受到缺料與漲價影響，達供需平衡仍需時間，稅後EPS為3.15元。 
網通規格升級為主要獲利引擎，800G Switch與低軌衛星產品挹注營收動能：展望2026~2027年，啟碁主要營收動能來
自歐美Service Provider與Enterprise客戶之Wi-Fi 7與5G FWA網通規格升級以及美系低軌衛星客戶 終端裝置部署量持
續提升。企業交換器業務方面，啟碁目前沒有計劃供應Data Center Switch予CSP，目前業務模式僅是透過Tier 1 Switch
供應商夥伴間接出貨予CSP客戶，主要原因為公司自身之資源集中於Campus Switch以及Neo-Cloud市場，目前已取得
相當不錯之進展與成果，下世代1.6T規格Switch以及Optical Circuit Switch(OCS)預計於2027年開始送樣，目標於2028
年開始貢獻營收，公司預估Switch業務在未來2至3年內可實現70~90%之年成⻑率。產能方面，目前公司主要生產基地
位於越南廠，產能比重達約6成，近期公司表示2026年累計之資本支出達30億元，主要用於越南擴廠和美國建廠需求，
4Q26為美國設廠關鍵期，是否設廠將視FCC、USMCA、關稅政策等政策以及客戶需求，針對涉及資安和國安相關列管
之產品線遷至美國生產，初期階段先從SKD半散裝料開始，穩定後推進至CKD全散裝料生產，未來不排除將電信運營商
相關製造服務移至美國據點。綜上所述，預估2026/2027年營收年增51.9%/24.3%至1,674.7/2,082.1億元，稅後EPS為
13.25/14.23元。

기아 (000270)

26.3Q Review: 수익성에 대한 입증이 필요  Company Brief

26.3Q Review: 매출액 31.6조원, 영업이익 1.8조원  
기아는 26.3Q 매출액 31.6조원 (+10.1% YoY, -4.4% QoQ), 영업이익 1.8조원 
(+24.3% YoY, -30.8% QoQ, +5.8% OPM) 등의 실적을 기록했다. 26.3Q 영업이익은 
시장 컨센서스 (2.36조원)를 22.9% 하회했다 [iM증권 추정치: 매출액 31.5조원, 
영업이익 2.3조원]. 주요 구성 요소별 내용은 아래와 같다. 
① P: 26.3Q 26.3Q 전동화 차량 판매는 30.0만대 (+47% YoY, 판매 비중 
36.2%)를 기록했는데, 이 중 HEV 17.2 만대 (+46% YoY), EV 12.3 만대 
(+75% YoY)로 구성됨. 미국 HEV 판매가 전년 동기 대비 118% 증가하며 
시장 성장률을 약 4배 상회했고, 특히 서유럽 EV 판매가 전년 대비 163% 
증가함. 단 26.3Q 중 원화가 빠르게 강세로 전환되며 원/달러, 원/유로 
평균 환율은 전분기 대비 각 5.2%, 6.2% 하락함에 따른 – 효과 관찰됨. 
② Q: 26.3Q 도매 판매는 84.7만대 (+7.9% YoY) 를 기록했고, 북미, 유럽, 
인도 등 주요 시장에서 고른 성장 나타남. 26.3Q 소매 판매 또한 82.9만대 
(+6.9% YoY)로 글로벌 산업 수요 (-6% YoY)를 상회. 북미는 HEV, 내수와 
유럽은 EV, 인도는 신형 셀토스와 소넷을 중심으로 판매가 확대됨 (이하 
26.3Q 권역별 도매 판매 YoY, 국내: +2.2%, 북미: +7.6%. 유럽: +19.3%. 
인도: +38.5%). 이란 사태 장기화로 아중동 도매 판매는 전년 동기 대비 
28.4% 감소했으나, 수요가 견조한 중남미와 CIS로 물량을 전환하며 
감소분을 상당 부분 만회함. 연간 도매 판매 가이던스 (335만대)를 유지함. 
③ C: 26.3Q 관세 비용은 약 0.7조원으로 추정되며, 전년 동기 대비 
5,000억원 감소함 (25.2Q 0.7조원, 25.3Q 1.2조원, 25.4Q 1.0조원, 26.1Q 
0.8조원). 반면 인센티브 증가 및 가격 인하 7,270억원, 서유럽 EV 비중 
확대에 따른 믹스 악화 4,650억원, 원자재 가격 상승 영향 3,680억원 등 
총 1.6조원의 감익 요인이 발생함. 이에 26.3Q 매출원가율은 84.1%로 
전년 동기 대비 3.0%p 상승함 (인센티브 +1.9%p, 재료비 +1.2%p). 다만 
26.3Q 기말 환율 급락하며 판매보증충당부채 환입 규모가 축소되었고 
판매보증비 약 9,000억원 감소. 판매관리비율은 10.1%로 3.7%p 개선됨. 
26.3Q 기아 순이익은 1.29조원으로 전년 동기 대비 9.6% 감소했다. 원화 강세에 
따라 외환환산 관련 이익이 전년 동기 540억원 이익에서 3,718억원으로 손실 
전환됐고, 현대모비스 등 관계사 이익 감소로 지분법손익 또한 360억원 감소했기 
때문이다 (현대차 순이익 감소 논리와 동일). 1) 산업 수요 감소와 중동 판매 차질에도 
권역별 파워트레인 대응을 통해 그룹사 대비 가지고 있는 외형 성장성을 증명했으나, 
2) 유럽 EV 판매 확대 과정에서 발생한 인센티브와 믹스 악화라는 비용 부담, 원자재 
가격 상승이 관세 감소 효과를 대부분 반납했고, 3) 분기 중 급격한 원화 강세가 
더해지며 시장 기대를 크게 하회하는 수익성을 기록했다는 것이 26.3Q 실적 총평이다. 
Buy (Maintain)

기아 (000270) 
2

실적 발표 후 주가 급락: 성장에도 불구하고 수익성에 대한 우려  
26.10.30 실적 발표 당일 기아 주가는 11.7% 하락했다. 하루 전 발표된 그룹사 실적 
발표와 상반된 모습이었다. 시장이 이번 실적에서 확인한 우려는 아래와 같다. 첫째, 
성장과 이익의 괴리다. 관세 영향을 제외한 26.3Q 영업이익은 2.52조원으로 전년 
동기(2.70조원) 대비 오히려 1,770억원 감소했다. 판매 증가에 따른 물량 효과 
(+4,420억원)와 환율 효과 (+5,290억원)가 발생했음에도, 인센티브와 믹스 악화로 이를 
대부분 반납한 것이다. 기아의 외형 성장 상당 부분이 유럽 EV 판매 증가에서 나오는데, 
해당 시장에서는 중국 EV 업체와의 경쟁으로 공격적인 프로모션이 불가피하다. 26.3Q 
유럽 권역에서의 중소형 EV 판매가 사업 계획을 크게 웃돌며 EV 믹스는 13.1%에서 
14.8%로 확대됐다. 시장은 이를 M/S를 위해 수익성을 훼손하고 있다고 받아들이는 
것이다. 둘째, 당장 26.4Q에도 유의미한 수익성 개선이 없을 것이라는 점이다. 기아는 
26.3Q 컨퍼런스 콜에서 4Q 영업이익률이 3Q 수준과 크게 다르지 않을 것이며, 연초 
제시한 연간 가이던스 (영업이익 10.2조원, +8.3% OPM) 에 다소 미달할 것이라고 
밝혔다. 중동 분쟁의 영향이 최소 연말까지 이어지고, 원자재와 물류비 부담 또한 
지속될 것이 회사의 판단이다. 무엇보다 원/달러 환율이 26.2Q 평균 약 1,505원에서 
26.3Q 약 1,430원, 현재 1,320~1,340원 수준까지 하락하며 이에 따른 - 영향이 커질 
수 있다. 셋째, 그룹사와의 이익 체력 비교다. 현대차는 26.3Q 파업과 생산 차질로 도매 
판매가 11.3% 감소했음에도 원가 절감을 통해 영업이익이 5.5% 증가했고, 시장 
기대를 상회했다. 반면 기아는 판매가 7.9% 증가했음에도 영업이익이 컨센서스를 20% 
이상 하회했다. 26.3Q 양사 영업이익 격차는 약 8,600억원까지 벌어진 상황이다.  
단기 노이즈를 제외한다면 기아의 기업 가치는 여전히 높다  
그럼에도 당사는 기아의 EV 전략, 낮은 수익성을 이유로 중장기 기업 가치에 대한 
기대를 낮출 이유는 없다고 판단한다. 첫째, 외부 요인을 걷어내면 본원적인 수익 
창출력은 유지되고 있다. 기아는 26.3Q 영업이익률을 낮춘 외부 요인을 원자재 1.2%p, 
중동 판매 차질 0.7%p, 환율 1.7%p 등 총 3.6%p로 분석했다. 즉 중동 분쟁이 
해소되면 상당 부분이 사라질 일회성 요인이다. 둘째, EV 수익성 개선 경로는 HEV가 
걸어온 길과 같다. 기아는 HEV 역시 도입 초기에는 수익을 내지 못했으나 현재는 ICE에 
버금가는 수익성을 내고 있다며, 26~27 년 현재의 EV 전략을 유지하겠다고 밝혔다. 
유럽 ICE 시장은 26.3Q 전년 동기 대비 약 20% 축소된 반면 EV 시장은 50% 이상 
성장하고 있다. 전환기에 스케일을 확보하지 못하면 EV 마진을 개선할 방법도 없다. 
기아는 향후 3년간 배터리, 모터, 히트펌프 등 주요 부품에서 연 10% 수준의 원가 
절감을 추진하고, 2~3년 내 EV를 HEV와 같은 수익성 경로에 올려놓겠다는 계획이다. 
셋째, 중장기 성장 계획을 수정하지 않았다. 기아는 27년 사업 계획이 CID 당시 제시한 
숫자와 큰 차이가 없을 것이며, 중장기 영업이익률 10% 목표도 유지했다. IAA 시행에 
대비해 슬로바키아 공장에서 EV를 전량 생산하고 ICE 국내 수출 방안도 검토 중이다. 
주주환원 측면에서도 TSR 35% 를 지키고, 자사주 매입보다 배당을 우선하겠다는 
입장이다. 10/30 종가와 최소 DPS 기준만으로도 배당수익률은 6.3%에 달하고 있다.

기아 판매관리비 (좌) 판매관리비율 (우)
  26.4Q 기아 매출액 31.8조원, 영업이익 2.2조원 전망 
기아 26.4Q 실적을 매출액 31.8조원 (+13.2% YoY), 영업이익 2.2조원 (+20.8% YoY, 
+7.0% OPM)으로 전망한다. 26.4Q 판매 성장세는 이어지고, 3Q에 집중 반영된 원자재 
부담 일부 완화와 계절적 성수기 효과로 3Q 대비 수익성은 소폭 개선될 것이다. 다만 
1) 원화 강세에 따른 평균 환율 하락, 2) 유럽 EV 인센티브의 높은 수준 유지 (미국은 
할부 금리 상승으로 QoQ 약 200달러 증가, 유럽은 전분기와 유사), 3) 중동 판매 
차질과 원자재, 물류비 부담 지속을 감안하면 의미 있는 반등은 어렵다. 이는 4Q 
영업이익률이 3Q 수준과 크게 다르지 않을 것이라는 회사의 언급과도 궤를 같이한다. 
기아 목표주가 150,000원으로 하향  
기아 목표주가를 26.4Q 추정치 하향을 반영해 150,000원으로 하향한다. 기아의 판매 
경쟁력은 금번 분기에도 증명됐다. 다만 EV 판매 확대가 수익성 개선으로 연결된다는 
점이 숫자로 확인되기 전까지, 시장은 기아의 성장에 할인율을 적용할 것이다. 중동 
분쟁 해소와 EV 원가 절감이 맞물리는 27년이 기아가 수익성을 증명할 시점이 될 
것이며, 6%를 상회하는 배당수익률이 그때까지 주가의 하방을 지지할 것으로 판단된다.

 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.

 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상 
산업추천 투자등급  
시가총액기준 산업별 시장비중대비 보유비중의 변화를 추천하는 것임 
 Overweight(비중확대) 
 Neutral(중립) 
 Underweight(비중축소)

[투자등급 비율 2026-09-30 기준] 
매수 중립(보유) 매도 
92% 8% -

자동차업종

◼ 3Q26 비용절감/환율 효과로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현 
◼ 2027년 물량–환율 영향 상쇄되며 2H27 이익체력 개선 본격화 예상 
◼ 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등에 주목

투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 50만원 하향(-6%) 
현대차 TP 50만원(시총 102조원)은 12MF EPS 41,978원에 Target PER 12배
적용하여 산출. 제한적인 실적 기대 반영해 타겟 밸류 하향함에 따라 TP 조정  
우려 대비 양호한 3Q26 실적으로 주가 하방 부담 완화. 주가의 추세적 상승 
위해서는 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등 확인돼야 할 
것. 기저효과에 따른 물량 개선이 주가 저점 받쳐줄 것이나, 현재 밸류(2027년 
PER 9.1배vs 글로벌 피어 6.1배)에서 추세 상승 이끌긴 어려울 것이기 때문. 
이에, 자동차 본업 외 1) 한/미국 Physical AI 투자 계획 및 2) BD 공급망 구축 
/양산 가시화에 따른 주가 반등 기대

3Q26 매출 45조(YoY -3%, 이하 YoY), OP 2.7조(+6%), OPM 5.9% 시현 
3Q26 OP 추정(2.5조)/컨센(2.4조) 상회. 파업에 따른 생산/판매 차질 영향으로 
기대 제한적이었으나, 비용 절감/환율 영향으로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현. 
[자동차] 매출 33조(-10%), OP 2.1조(+25%), OPM 6.5%. 도매 판매 92.1만대
(-11%). 판매 부진과 원자재 부담에도 불구, 환효과(+1.3조)/비용절감(+0.5조)
로 실적 방어. 이외 YoY 변수별 영향은 물량(-1.1조)/믹스(-0.6조)/원자재(-
0.5조). [금융] 매출 9.2조(+28%), OP 4,710억(-18%), OPM 5.1%, [기타] 매출 
2.9조(+2%), OP 712억(-72%), OPM 2.5% 기록

2027년 매출 191조(YoY +2%, 이하 YoY), OP 12.7조(+15%), OPM 6.6% 전망 
기저효과 바탕으로 연결 도매 물량 YoY +5% 증가 예상하나, 원달러 환율 -
4% 하락(가정 환율 1,380원)하며 1H27 → 2H27로가며 이익체력 개선 예상 
(단위: 십억원, %)

### Page 5

DAISHIN SECURITIES

5  
  현대차 3Q26 실적 컨퍼런스콜 QnA 
Q. 금융부문 환율 변동에 따른 4Q26 매출 및 손익 영향 
- 금융부문 환율 영향은 현대차 실적에 미치는 환율 영향과 동일한 방향으로 발생

Q. 3Q26 금융부문 수익성 악화 요인 
- 장기금리 상승에 따른 조달 및 이자비용 증가가 주요인. 자산/부채 만기 매칭에 따른 
금리 상승 영향 반영 
- EV 중심 중고차 가격지수 소폭 하락 및 차량 반환에 따른 매각손실, 금리 상승에 따
른 연체율 및 신용비용 상승 
- 차량 판매가격 인하 영향은 없으며, 3Q26 도매 판매 감소에 따른 금융 영업수익 영향 
불가피했음

Q. 향후 주주환원 정책에서 배당 및 자사주 매입 우선순위 
- 기존 밸류업 정책: 최소 DPS 1만원/TPR 35% 제시. 연간 배당 약 2.6조원 → TPR 
35% 충족 위한 잔여분 자사주 매입/소각 활용 
- 경영환경 변화 고려하여 기존 밸류업 프로그램 전반의 주주가치 제고 방안 재검토중 
- 새로운 정책 확정 및 주주 소통 전까지 기존 약속인 최소 DPS 1만원 유지할 계획 
- 시장의 배당 및 자사주 매입 간 균형 요구를 인지하고 있으며, 주주 의견 수렴 후 주
주가치 제고 계획 공유 예정

Q. 3Q26 영업이익 증감 요인 중 기타 +6,400억 발생 원인 
- 월별 컨틴전시 활동을 통한 비용 최적화 및 재료비 절감 지속 추진 
- 4Q26 원가 절감 효과는 3Q26 대비 확대 예상

Q. 3Q26 영업외손익 감소 원인 
- 3Q26 환율 급락으로 외화 매출채권 등 외화자산 관련 외화환산손실 및 외환거래손실 
발생 
- 매출채권 인식 시점과 회수 시점 간 환율 차이에 따른 외환차손 발생 
- 지분법 대상 기업들의 3Q26 실적 부진에 따른 지분법손익 감소도 일부 영향. 대부분 
외환 관련 손실에서 발생

Q. 중국 OEM 국내 진출 확대에 따른 대응 및 신차/가격 전략 
- 중국 OEM 및 테슬라의 국내 M/S 확대 대응중. 다만, 잦은 가격 조정은 중고차 잔존
가치 및 고객가치 훼손할 수 있어 지양 
- 현대캐피탈의 캡티브 금융 경쟁력 활용. 잔존가치 유예할부 등을 통해 월 납입금을 낮
춰 중국 OEM 대비 경쟁력 확보 
- 서비스 네트워크 기반으로 경쟁사 대비 경쟁력 확보 노력 
- 국내 신차: 4Q26 아반떼 HEV, 투싼, GV90, G80 HEV, 싼타페 FL(2026.12월), G90 
FL 등 출시 예정 
- 북미: 4Q26 투싼, GV80 HEV 출시 및 27년 초 GV90 출시 예상 
- 유럽: 아이오닉3 판매 개시, 4Q26 투싼/베이온 등 주요 모델 출시 예정 
- 중국: 아이오닉 V / 인도: 베이온, 아이오닉 P 등 출시 예정 
- 신차 출시 계획 연도별로 다수 예정. 가격은 원가/경쟁사/소비자 가격 수용성 등을 고
려해 결정

### Page 6

Q. 3Q26 법인세비용 증가 원인 및 4Q26 전망 
- 일회성 요인보다는 법인별 손익 믹스 및 적용 세율 차이에 따른 영향 
- 4Q26 법인세 수준은 QoQ 큰 변동 없을 것으로 전망

- Overweight(비중확대)

- Neutral(중립) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률과 유사한 수준 예상 
- Underweight(비중축소)

  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 -10%p~10%p 주가 변동 예상

TUẦN 26-30/10 [Điểm nhấn thị trường] 
VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -32.53 điểm tương ứng -1.83%, với thanh 
khoản suy giảm

[Nhận định xu hướng] 
Mặc dù rủi ro mở rộng thêm nhịp điều chỉnh vẫn còn để ngỏ, tuy nhiên khi xu 
hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được bảo lưu, VNINDEX được kỳ vọng sẽ cho phản 
ứng hồi phục trở lại tại các điểm đỡ bên dưới

### Page 2

2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -32.53 điểm tương ứng -1.83%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Linh kiện ô tô, Dầu khí và Công nghệ là các nhóm ngành có m ức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -634 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, ACB và VPL; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-609 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng -5,829 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -1,462 hợp đồng. Tự doanh duy trì mua ròng 722 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -5,278 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam đánh mất đà hồi phục của tuần trước, khi VN-Index quay đầu giảm khoảng 
1,8% về đóng cửa quanh 1.742 điểm, với phiên cuối tuần có thời điểm lùi sâu về sát 1.727 
điểm trước khi thu hẹp đà giảm. Dù mùa báo cáo KQKD quý 3 diễn ra tương đối tích cực, 
tuy nhiên các lo ngại về việc môi trường lãi suất cao vẫn duy trì trong bối cảnh căng thẳng 
Trung Đông leo thang đang đè nặng lên tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Kết quả kỳ họp của FED trong 
tuần qua nhìn chung phù hợp với kỳ vọng và không gây ra ảnh hưởng đáng kể. Điểm kém 
tích cực nhất là khối ngoại đã quay lại bán ròng với tổng giá trị khoảng 2.700 tỷ đồng 
trong tuần — đảo ngược diễn biến mua ròng cuối tuần trước, cho thấy đà rút vốn ngoại 
vẫn chưa thực sự dừng lại.

Quan điểm kỹ thuật 
Xu hướng & Hành động

Chỉ số VNINDEX

Source: Trading View, KB Securities Vietnam

Ngắn hạn 
(Dưới 3 tháng)

Trung hạn (Từ 3 đến 9 tháng)

Chỉ báo  Nhận định xu hướng Hành động 
Xanh đậm  Khả năng tăng điểm cao Mua mạnh 
Xanh nhạt  Khả năng tăng điểm vừa phải Mua từng phần 
Vàng  Xu hướng trung tính Mua/Bán trading 
Cam  Khả năng giảm điểm vừa phải Bán từng phần 
Đỏ  Khả năng giảm điểm cao Bán mạnh

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tuần qua hình thành mẫu nến giảm trên đồ thị tuần, nhưng chưa thoái lui quá 1 
nửa cây nến tăng của tuần trước, cùng thanh khoản được tiết giảm, hàm ý nhịp điều chỉnh 
vẫn trong phạm vi cho phép. Trên khung ngày, sau bốn phiên giằng co đầu tuần với thanh 
khoản thấp, chỉ số đã chịu áp lực sụt giảm mạnh hơn trong phiên cuối tuần, chớm xuống 
dưới đường tín hiệu chính giữa dải Bollinger, nhưng cho tín hiệu rút chân ngay sau đó, thể 
hiện áp lực bán tháo đã tạm thời được kiểm soát. Mặc dù rủi ro mở rộng thêm nhịp điều 
chỉnh vẫn còn để ngỏ, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được bảo lưu, VNINDEX 
được kỳ vọng sẽ cho phản ứng hồi phục trở lại tại các điểm đỡ bên dưới.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1850 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1800 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1720 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1680 (+-15)

### Page 4

4

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu 
Mã Giá đóng cửa tuần Biến động từ đầu tháng Giá khuyến nghị mua Giá mục tiêu Lợi nhuận  kỳ vọng Điểm nhấn đầu tư Báo cáo phân tích 
FRT 148.70 1.2% 143.40 165.00 15.1% 
- Sau khi phủ sóng các thành phố lớn, chuỗi Long Châu đang mở rộng sang các vùng cấp 2, 3. 
Năm 2026 dự kiến mở thêm 400-450 cửa hàng (+20%YoY) với DT trên mỗi cửa hàng cũ tăng 
trưởng 6%-8%/năm 
- Dư địa lấy thêm thị phần từ các cửa hàng nhỏ lẻ vẫn còn tương đối rộng, FRT đặt mục tiêu 
chiếm 50% thị phần doanh thu ngành bán lẻ dược phẩm, từ mức 17%-20% hiện tại 
- Tiềm năng dài hạn từ mảng tiêm chủng tương đối tích cực 
FRT 
MWG 74.00 3.6% 76.70 121.60 58.5% 
- Chuỗi TGDD+ĐMX tiếp tục duy trì tăng trưởng trung bình 2 chữ số trong 2026 nhờ chu kỳ nâng 
cấp đồ điện tử, chu kỳ Elnino và nhu cầu BĐS bàn giao 
- Số lượng cửa hàng mở mới của BHX có thể đạt 1000 cửa hàng trong 2026 (+25% YoY) với tăng 
trưởng DT trên cửa hàng cũ 2026/2027 đạt 14%/10% 
- Khả năng tăng doanh thu tài chính đến từ việc tận dụng phần phải trả nhà sản xuất/cung ứng 
sẽ tỷ lệ thuận với quy mô DT

The Week Ahead October 23, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         2

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Bank of Thailand (BOT) (October 28)

We expect the BOT to maintain its policy 
rate at 1.00% at its 28 October meeting, 
extending its pause for a fourth 
consecutive decision as it balances 
inflationary pressures against uneven 
and subdued economic growth. Our view 
is aligned with fixed market income 
pricing. Headline inflation rose anew to 
2.8% yoy in September 2026, driven by 
higher energy, food, and core price 
increases, but remained within the upper 
end of the central bank’s 1 -3% target 
range. As the BOT is still likely to look 
through what it views as temporary, 
supply-driven inflationary pressu res, it 
will likely refrain from tightening policy, 
and keep interest rates accommodative 
to support the soft and uneven growth 
cycle.

European Central Bank (October 29)

We expect the ECB to extend its pause, 
keeping the deposit facility rate at 2.5%. 
Market participants will instead focus on 
the central bank’s assessment of rising 
French and Italian bond yields and 
widening spreads versus Bunds. While 
we do not expect dir ect references to 
individual countries, the ECB is likely to 
signal that recent developments in 
France reflect the country’s weaker 
fiscal and debt profile rather than a 
broader systemic concern. Policymakers 
will seek to differentiate between a 
localised bond sell -off and a Eurozone -
wide market disruption, justifying their 
decision to monitor developments rather 
than implement specific measures at this 
juncture. Meanwhile, inflation continues 
to rise, with little relief expected in 
upcoming readings given elevated 
global fuel prices. 
Bank of Japan (October 30)

The Bank of Japan is expected to keep 
the overnight call rate unchanged at 1.25% 
at this meeting, pausing after the 25bp 
hike in September. The BOJ is likely to 
assess the impact of the previous rate 
hike before taking further action. The 
recent stabilisat ion of the yen also 
reduces the urgency of another hike at 
this meeting.

The tightening bias should remain intact. 
Growth and inflation remain broadly on 
track, leaving room for the BOJ to revise 
up its FY2026 –FY2027 GDP and CPI 
forecasts. Wage growth has also 
remained strong, with Rengo reportedly 
targeting wage increases of 6% in 2027, 
reinforcing the BOJ’s confidence that 
underlying inflation can be sustained 
around its 2% target. We continue to 
expect the policy rate to rise to 1.50% in 
December 2026 or January 2027, 
followed by a further increase to 1.75% 
by 2Q 2027.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Hong Kong SAR

Export growth is expected to accelerate 
from 53.0% yoy in August to 55.0% in 
September, in line with the pickup in 
China’s export growth from 25.0% to 
36.3%. As a key re-export hub for China,

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 23, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

Hong Kong should benefit from robust 
AI-related electronics trade, with 
electrical machinery, equipment and 
parts exports to Hong Kong accounting 
for 20.6% of China’s total exports. 
Import growth is also expected to remain 
firm, supported by resilient expo rt 
orders and gradually improving 
investment sentiment, sustaining 
demand for intermediate and capital 
goods.

Singapore

Singapore’s industrial production (IP) 
likely remained in expansion for a 13th 
consecutive month in September 2026, 
although we expect growth to moderate 
to 7.6% yoy from August’s strong 15.4% 
yoy pace. IP growth continued to be 
underpinned by the electronics and 
precision engineering clusters, which 
has been key drivers of Singapore’s 
manufacturing strength, reflecting 
sustained demand for artificial 
intelligence-related hardware. However, 
performance across clusters remained 
uneven, with chemicals and biomedical 
manufacturing likely contracting, and 
tempering overall gains.

South Korea

The preliminary estimate for 3Q26 GDP 
is likely to show growth remaining solid 
at 3.2% yoy , slightly below 3.7% in 2Q 
and 3.8% in 1Q. Nominal GDP growth is 
likely to remain in double digits, 
significantly outpacing real GDP growth, 
thanks to improved terms of trade amid 
rising memory export prices. By 
expenditure, exports picked up in 3Q in 
both nominal and real terms, supported 
by stronger semiconductor shipments. 
Investment also appeared robust, as 
reflected in the equipment investment 
estimation index and machinery order 
index. Private consumption, however, 
appeared to weaken amid the sharp 
KOSPI correction since July following  
the introduction of leveraged ETF 
regulations. Our full -year GDP growth 
forecasts of 3.5% for 2026 and 2.4% for 
2027 remain on track.

Taiwan

The preliminary estimate for 3Q GDP is 
likely to show growth remaining strong 
at 12 –13% yoy , close to 12.9% in 2Q, 
albeit below the peak of 15.4% in 1Q. 
Exports maintained strong double -digit 
growth in 3Q (44.6% yoy  in nominal 
terms and 21.1% in real terms), supported 
by robust shipments of semiconductors, 
servers and other AI -related products. 
Machinery investment also sustained 
strong double-digit growth, underpinned 
by higher capacity utilisation in the ICT 
sector and a resulting increase in capital 
goods imports. Private consumption 
appeared to have strengthened further, 
supported by stock market gains, 
improving wage growth, and the 
associated wealth and income effects. 
Construction investment remained the 
weak s pot, weighed down by elevated 
inventories in the new-home market and 
the ongoing need for inventory digestion. 
We maintain our 2026 and 2027 GDP 
growth forecasts at 11.6% and 5.6%, 
respectively, with risks tilted to the 
upside.

Report Introduction
Our proprietary dbDataInsights survey shows cyber threats are evolving faster than organisations can adapt
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.
“It’s the only thing that can shut down my business.”
That was the punchline from a conversation I had recently with the CEO of a large
privately held financial services company. This was his comment when I followed up

as to why cybersecurity risk is the number one concern that “keeps him up at night”.
To help understand this risk, our dbDataInsights team surveyed more than 50
cybersecurity experts. The uncomfortable conclusion is that many firms are
unprepared for cyber threats. Cyber risk is increasing as AI adoption accelerates.
—89% agree the threat landscape is evolving faster than organisations can adapt.
—78% say the gap between highly and poorly prepared organisations is widening.

What has changed with AI advancement? Cyberattacks have become faster,
cheaper, more deceptive and more scalable. Capabilities once reserved for
sophisticated actors are now broadly accessible to a new generation of profit-driven
criminals. At the same time, rising geopolitical tension has made cyber operations
an increasingly attractive low-cost, deniable tool for nation states. Corporates and
investors therefore need to understand their broader exposure and critical

infrastructure risk in their supply chain and with stakeholders.
Among other autonomous incidents, the recent Hugging Face incident
highlighted AI’s cyberattack potential. AI agents caused harm without
direction from a human attacker, with weak guardrails and no human
judgement to restrain them. Beyond the systemic questions it raised, the attack
highlights three additional concerns: (i) AI operates at a speed and scale humans
cannot match, with agents taking over 17,000+ actions in four days; (ii) the intrusion

began in the data-processing pipeline, where AI platforms are uniquely exposed;
and (iii) the agents escaped their test environment through a widely used third-party
tool representing supplier risk in an unfamiliar form.

### Page 4

Report summary and notable takeaways
Secular trends and AI adoption have widened the attack surface; the severity of losses is compounding
Source: FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3), company security advisories, OECD, company reports, Deutsche Bank Research.
Secular trends have widened the attack surface. This includes rapid
digitalisation, widespread Internet of Things (IoT) endpoints, hybrid workforces,
cloud adoption and deeper supply chain dependencies. The scale of the threat

increase is reflected in data from the FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3),
which shows cybercrime complaints more than doubled from 2019 pre-pandemic
baseline of ~467,000 to ~1.0mn in 2025, with a 3x expansion relative to 2017 levels.
Barriers to entry are lower; attack tools are now sold “off the shelf”. Today,
cybercrime marketplaces, available on the dark web, look less like the underworld
portrayed in films and more like an online software store. Buyers can access AI-

powered tools that scan for software vulnerabilities (see top-right chart showing
reported CVEs after the release of cyber capable frontier models), write malicious
code at speed, and provide access to malware that rewrites its own code to avoid
detection as it runs. Ransomware-as-a-service (RaaS) takes it further, renting out
ready-made attack kits, often in exchange for a portion of the profits.
Impersonation is becoming harder to detect. Hackers can now generate more

realistic phishing messages, while voice cloning and deepfakes make impersonation
convincing enough to gain access unnoticed. With stolen credentials, attackers often
aren’t hacking in, they are simply logging in.
The rise of loose cybercriminal collectives. Some of the most damaging recent
attacks have been linked to loosely organised, often English-speaking networks such
as ShinyHunters and Scattered Spider. The latter has been widely named as the

group behind the 2025 Marks & Spencer cyberattack, in which the hacking group
deployed RaaS. AI is enabling these groups to become larger and faster, widening
the gap between the speed of attack and a defender’s response.
The severity of losses is compounding. A 2026 IBM study puts the average breach
at a record USD ~5.0mn, up 12% YoY, but the impact on large companies can run
into hundreds of millions or even billions. Estimates for global cyber insurance

premiums (per Munich Re) point to a rise from roughly USD 7.0bn in 2020 to USD
15.0bn in 2025, with expectations to reach USD 28.0bn by 2030.

### Page 5

5
Among the more concerning survey takeaways is that a large-scale and
significant systemic incident is now the base case.
— 83% expect a significant systemic attack within two years and 81% expect it to be
moderately, very, or extremely severe for economic activity and markets.
— 81% say AI favours attackers today, with not one respondent putting defenders
ahead of attackers. Only 20% expect defenders to be ahead in five years.
Geopolitical tensions coupled with rising cyber capabilities have transformed

cyberspace into a digital battlefield between global powers. Intent has moved
towards espionage and disruption of not only critical infrastructure (e.g., healthcare,
finance, power, industrial systems, defence) but private companies as well. Chinese
President Xi Jinping has linked cybersecurity to national security and economic and
social stability, with China’s public statements similarly emphasising the protection
of critical infrastructure. At the same time, the US’s June 2025 Executive Order on

Cybersecurity reflects its continued focus on national cyber resilience.
Europe relies on US cyber tools while its start-ups lag in funding. Reliance on US
security vendors exposes European firms to potential access, update or export
restrictions over the long-term. Shrinking this gap will prove difficult given US cyber
start-ups attract nearly 6x the venture and growth funding of European peers. And
while the timing of quantum computing (which could break widely used encryption

methods) remains uncertain, it could further deepen Europe’s dependency.
Ultimately, cyber preparedness is a financial variable. Investors should ask what
firms spend on security versus AI adoption, how well they know their vulnerabilities,
and how fast they can recover.
— Only 24% of respondents think organisations have sufficient visibility into the
threats relevant to them, even though 57% saw cybersecurity budgets rise over
the past 12 months.

— 81% agree that cybersecurity related laws and regulation cannot keep pace, and
65% doubt leadership understands cyber risk well enough.

### Page 6

6
When cyber weakness = share price consequence…
Investors are more aware of cyber risk today, but they rarely sell a stock due to
perceived cyber vulnerabilities (although we are aware of some who have).
—What is the potential impact? Our dbDataInsights event study of 47 breaches
shows a cumulative breach-attributed return of c. -2.9% within the first two
trading days and c. -6.0% through first 20 days trading days. The erosion then
continues more gradually, reaching c. -6.7% nine months after disclosure (see

slide 13).
Which industries are most vulnerable?
—Our survey respondents point to (1) government & public sector (52%), (2)
financial services (50%), (3) energy and utilities (46%) and (4) healthcare and life
sciences follow (39%, rising to 48% among US respondents).
We should caveat, that high sector exposure does not mean every company in it is
equally vulnerable. Some firms in these “exposed” sectors may very well be better
prepared given they have built specialist teams and defence protocols for decades.

What about access to credit and ratings? Credit and rating providers are paying
attention. Moody’s and S&P both cut their outlook on Jaguar Land Rover within two
months of its 2025 hack. Today, this type of impact is not widespread. But if risk
escalates through a systemic incident or an AI-driven step change in attacks these
mechanisms, including ESG ratings, could reprice asset ownership or impact access
to credit. In fact, some lenders are already pricing it. Journal of Corporate Finance

research using US syndicated loan data found borrowers with persistently higher cyber
risk pay 4–13bp more.
The bottom line? While a broader systemic attack is hard to prepare for, corporates
and investors should be asking questions about vulnerabilities, security investment,
preparedness and recovery should an attack occur. This is especially true for
industries quickly adopting AI capabilities and / or a high threat landscape.

dbDataInsights Cybersecurity Expert Survey methodology
—This survey was conducted among 54 cybersecurity professionals across the US
and Europe in September of 2026. Questions took the form of either multiple

choice selections or free form responses. The dbDataInsights team received the
responses anonymously, but respondents were required to work or consult in
cyber security roles
—Who took the survey? On self-identified roles, around 80% sit inside an
organisation’s cybersecurity function, with the remainder working independently
(15%) or in academia and research (6%). The most common titles were security
architect (23%), Chief Information Security Officer (16%) and security analyst (9%),

alongside Head of Security, Deputy CISO, Incident Response Lead and Security
Operations Lead.
—Only a small minority of respondents work for cyber resellers, thus survey views
are primarily attributable to those working within an organization on
cybersecurity.
—Survey participants were also asked for their perspectives on cyber resilience and
governance, what is misunderstood, alongside emerging threats, risk to AI
adoption and the influence of geopolitics.

—While responses were anonymous, in some cases we conducted follow up
interviews with certain participants where contact details were made available.
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.

Risk from AI-enabled attacks and added AI adoption vulnerabilities
Insider threats and supply chain compromises remain a growing concern
“The fear of AI writing undetectable malware is … overblown. Attackers were
never short of malware, since they can buy it ready made along with initial
access. Good detection looks at behaviour rather than exact code, so a rewritten
payload still has to do the same suspicious things.”
“Frontier AI models are currently the main hype. Automated phishing and

scams are underappreciated, yet they are responsible for the bigger
monetary losses at the moment.”
“There are too many organisations relying on the same providers for
critical services and this means single points of failure.”
“A corporation might be very secure but if they rely on a third party that
does not have the same level of security posture, [it] could be
compromised via this third-party (basically their weak link).”
“Existing employee login behaviour and usage of AI is underappreciated –

it’s what poses the biggest threat to a breach.”
“No company can guarantee they won’t be breached, so there needs to
be more focus put on the ability to recover and survive an attack.”
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research .

### Page 11

11
Experts believe governments are not keeping pace, and no one owns AI oversight
How experts surveyed by dbDataInsights view governance and regulation
—AI adoption is outpacing internal controls. As one expert put it: “Companies
are pushing the use of AI more to their employees without the proper
mechanisms and policies in place to prevent misuse or attacks initiated by the AI
systems themselves.”
—Regulation is struggling to keep pace. 81% of respondents agree that

cybersecurity-related laws and regulations are failing to keep up with
technological change, and 54% say regulatory requirements can improve security
but may also slow innovation. One respondent highlighted “the levels of
uncertainty, the long running nature of the problems, and the rapidity of change.”
—Responsibility for AI oversight remains unsettled. International bodies are the
leading choice, supported by 50% of respondents. No option commands a

majority, underscoring the lack of consensus on who should oversee AI
development.
—Better board questions may not guarantee better decisions. The survey
points to more informed questioning by corporate leadership, yet 65% doubt that
leadership teams understand cyber risk well enough to make informed business
trade-offs. Measuring activity more effectively than outcomes further complicates
oversight.
—Accountability is becoming personal. Only 43% say accountability for cyber risk

is clearly defined in their governance framework, yet the consequences now reach
the top as a handful of CEOs have left or gone into early retirement after
financially consequential cyber breaches.
Who should primarily oversee AI development?

Material cyber breaches can cause a notable drop in share price
Our dbDataInsights study found a 2.9% drop on average within two trading days, with the cumulative breach-attributed return of -6.0% on trading day 20.

Source: dbDataInsights, Bloomberg Finance LP., Deutsche Bank Research, Company filings. Event study of reported cybersecurity incidents, 2015 -2025, 34 US and 13 European companies across nine sectors.
What typically happens to a company’s shares when a breach is reported?
—Short-term impact: The market prices the breach immediately. Our model shows
cumulative breach-attributed (i.e. isolating only the part of the share price move
we can pin on the breach itself) returns are c. -2.9% within two trading days of

at one year, although the one-year point estimate (our single best-guess figure)
should be treated with caution given the width of the confidence band.
—Pre-breach price behaviour: We find no evidence of information leakage ahead
of disclosure. While the breach component for the [-10,-1] window (ten trading
days before the news broke) is +0.04%, the corresponding t-stat value of 0.03 falls
notably below the usual bar for significance (c.2). As such, we conclude the market

had not begun reacting before the announcement.
Event study methodology
—Sample: 47 publicly reported cybersecurity incidents across the US and Europe
over 2015-2025, measured against each company’s regional sector benchmark.
Requirement of 5 years of stock returns pre-attack and 1 year of post-attack.
—Day 0: the day an incident was first publicly reported or disclosed.
—Baseline: We use five years of pre-incident share-price performance to
estimate: (i) Beta (β): how sensitive a company’s returns are to movements in its

sector and (ii) Alpha (α): the extent to which the company typically outperforms
or underperforms its sector after adjusting for that sensitivity.
—Attribution: post-incident returns are split into sector-relative returns, alpha
(α) and breach (abnormal returns attributed to the disclosure).
—95% confidence interval: Range within which we can say with 95% confidence
that the real share price impact lies, based on our sample data.
Total return attribution

Marks & Spencer (Reported Apr 2025)
What happened?
A breach via an attack on a connected 3rd
party forced the suspension of all online
orders and led to empty store shelves.
What did it cost?
~£136mn of direct costs related to the
attack, estimated group operating profit
impact of around £300mn for the FY. M&S
also said they had received £100mn via
cyber insurance.
Coupang (Reported Nov 2025)
What happened?
A data breach exposed the records of
more than 30mn users with a former

employee reportedly behind the attack.
What did it cost?
The firm was fined more than ~625bn won
($409mn) (2/3rd directly attributable to
data leak), and also announced a $1.2bn
voucher compensation scheme.
UnitedHealth (Reported Feb 2024)
What happened?
Hackers reportedly used stolen credentials
to access a subsidiary’s (Change
Healthcare) systems, stealing data and
deploying ransomware.
What did it cost?
Total costs exceeded $3bn, with the CEO
summoned to testify before Congress,

where he confirmed that a $22mn ransom
was paid.
Equifax (Reported Sep 2017)
What happened?
A data breach saw hackers expose
sensitive customer information, including
145.5mn Social Security numbers and
more than 200k credit card numbers.
Attackers reportedly moved inside Equifax
systems for 76 days before being detected.
What did it cost?
Settlements, fines and legal costs
exceeded $1bn, while the firm also spent
more than $1.5bn on tech upgrades

following the incident.

Notable cyber attacks: how they happened, estimated costs and share price reaction, cont.
Nike (Reported Jan 2026)
What happened?
A cybercrime group leaked what it claimed
was 1.4TB of internal Nike data (appearing
to cover metrics such as product
specifications and pricing), following expiry
of a ransom deadline.
What did it cost?
While Nike did confirm it was investigating
the incident, it did not report any
operational disruption. Underperformance

shown in the chart does not appear to be
related to the breach.
T-Mobile (Reported Aug 2021)
What happened?
An attacker reportedly targeted an
exposed router as an entry point, before
using brute force attacks to gain further
access to T-Mobile systems.
What did it cost?
In 2022 the firm agreed to pay $500mn to
settle a class-action lawsuit ($350mn
payment to class members  plus $150mn
on security spend) but has since suffered
further data breaches, contributing to an

additional $31.5mn regulatory settlement.
Coinbase (Reported May 2025)
What happened?
Attackers bribed at least one support
agent at a connected 3rd party to access
Coinbase support systems and steal
customer data, then tricked users into
sending crypto.
What did it cost?
Coinbase refused to pay the $20mn
ransom demanded by the attackers to
keep the attack quiet. Instead they
reimbursed affected users and cited costs
of up to $400mn. The stock impact was

short-lived, though MSCI applied an ESG
score deduction.
Jaguar Land Rover (Reported Sep 2025)
What happened?
Production was shut down for five weeks
due to what was deemed a ransomware
attack; though no ransom was asked for
(later reporting suggested potential Russia
links were being investigated).
What did it cost?
JLR posted £485mn Q2’ FY26 loss (vs.
£398mn profit in Q2’ FY25), with an
additional £196mn in “cyber-related costs”.
These impacts filtered through to the

share price of JLR’s Parent (Tata Motors),
while UK economic impacts were
estimated at £1.9bn.

### Page 16

16
Targets go beyond tech and can also impact critical infrastructure
MGM Resorts (Reported Sep 2023)
What happened?
A vishing call to the IT help desk was reportedly used to reset credentials, after which
ransomware took down hotel systems, bookings and casino floor operations for several
days.
What did it cost?
MGM declined to pay a ransom. Around $100mn hit to third-quarter 2023 results plus
roughly ~$10mn of additional one-time expenses related to the incident.

Caesars Entertainment (Reported Sep 2023)
What happened?
The same social-engineering group as in the MGM incident targeted an outsourced IT
support vendor, gaining access to the loyalty programme database and member data.
What did it cost?
Caesars paid roughly $15mn of a reported $30mn demand and kept customer-facing
operations running.
NHS / Synnovis (Reported Jun 2024)
What happened?
Ransomware against pathology provider Synnovis significantly reduced its capacity to

process tests leading to delays / cancellations of over 11,000 appointments.
What did it cost?
Estimated 2024 direct operational financial impact of around GBP 32mn for Synnovis,
several times the profit reported in the prior year, plus wider NHS disruption costs.
Colonial Pipeline (Reported May 2021)
What happened?
A compromised VPN credential without multi-factor authentication allowed attackers to
access the network where they subsequently deployed ransomware. The operator shut the

pipeline down, triggering fuel shortages (c. 70% of gas stations in some states ran out of
fuel) across the US East Coast.
What did it cost?
A $4.4mn ransom was paid, of which about $2.3mn was later recovered by the US
authorities.
Bitget (Reported Sep 2026)
What happened?
Attackers exploited a vulnerability in a 3rd party security product to steal internal network
credentials, forge withdrawal instructions, bypass risk controls and ultimately steal crypto.

What did it cost?
An estimated $388mn worth of funds was transferred out by the attackers. While the firm
reimbursed affected users via its User Protection Fund, there were significant outflows
following the incident as users moved to take funds off the platform.
Progress Software (Reported May 2023)
What happened?
Attackers exploited a zero-day SQL injection vulnerability in Progress Software’s MOVEit file
transfer system. This was then used by attackers to steal data from firms running the

Debbie Jones | dbDataInsights | Under Attack | October 2026

### Page 17

Cyber breaches can impact consumer behaviour
Roughly 4 in 10 consumers we surveyed say they would stop buying from a company they shop with after a known breach or attack.
Source: dbDataInsights monthly consumer survey, Deutsche Bank Research. EU Average includes France, Germany, Italy and Spain.
How will customers respond to a data breach once it occurs?
dbDataInsights survey of consumer responses, % of respondents
How will customers react once the breach is resolved?

Consumer responses among those who would either stop buying or were unsure, % of respondents
Consumer surveys suggest that cyber breaches can undermine customer
loyalty.  Results of our dbDataInsights Consumer survey show the potential
mindset of a consumer after a cyber breach occurs:
—Across the US, UK and EU average, 39–41% say they would stop buying, while only
27–28% would continue. Around a third are unsure.
—Among those who would stop buying or are unsure, only 18–22% say they would

return as normal. Another 30–35% would buy selected products only, 17–20%
would not return, and 28–29% remain unsure. Note that this implies a range of
13-15% believe they would not shop at the store again after the breach is
resolved.
Admittedly, these stated intentions may overstate actual switching, particularly for
essential purchases where alternatives are limited. Nevertheless, the findings
suggest that cyber breaches that put customer privacy at risk can influence

purchasing decisions, especially if they have alternatives.

dbDataInsights Consumer Survey Methodology
—Our proprietary survey conducted in 2026 (January – September) asked

consumers how they would respond if a company they shopped at regularly
was involved in a data breach or cyberattack, and how they would respond
once the disruption was resolved. This monthly survey covers households and
consumers in Germany, France, Italy, Spain, the United Kingdom, and the
United States.
—For each country referenced above, a nationally representative sample of more
than 500 households is surveyed each month (over 600 households in the US).

Collectively, this meant over the course of the survey (January – September
2026), we surveyed almost 30,000 households.

### Page 18

18
Material cyber incidents have contributed to negative credit outlooks
The infrastructure exists for cyberattacks to have a greater impact on credit quality should cyber disruption continue to accelerate
Source: S&P, Moody’s, Fitch, Deutsche Bank Research.
—Credit rating agencies consider both pre-breach resilience frameworks and
post-breach financial impacts. Agencies assess pre-breach cyber preparedness
through a lens of mitigating risk, while post-breach credit impacts centre on

financial capacity to withstand the resulting shock (considers scale and length of
the disruption); highly leveraged issuers with limited liquidity are seen as more
vulnerable. Negative outlook changes at JLR and Equifax illustrate the potential
credit impact. Fitch generally considers permanent reductions in cash generation
more credit-significant than temporary cash-flow delays.
—Ratings agencies are moving towards continuous monitoring. In 2021,

Moody’s invested $250mn in BitSight, which produces daily security ratings.
Moody’s has since built BitSight’s ratings into its credit tools, including Credit
Catalyst and EDF-X, for uses such as underwriting and ongoing monitoring of
borrowers and suppliers. This means lenders can see a company’s exposed
vulnerabilities as a potential indicator of corporate cyber risk.
S&P Global:
“The increasing frequency of cyberattacks and the potential for rapid deterioration in credit

profiles after an attack are risk factors relevant for our rating assessments.”
Moody’s:
“Cyberattacks can undermine the creditworthiness of companies, organizations and
governments, across all sectors and geographies, hitting revenues, increasing costs, and eroding
customer trust.”
Fitch Ratings:
“Although cyber events cannot be predicted with great accuracy, the associated risk can be
managed through proactive strategies, technology, policies, and vigilance.”

“The chief executive officer and board of directors hold ultimate responsibility for managing
cyber risk.”
Example rating actions where cyber was a named factor

Cyber governance & breaches can impact ESG scores
Sectors that hold the most personal data, and monetise it most directly, carry the greatest risk weighting in mainstream ESG scores
Source: MSCI ESG Ratings Methodology, Morningstar Sustainalytics ESG Risk Ratings, SASB Materiality Map, Deutsche Bank Resear ch.
Cybersecurity is generally seen as both a social and governance consideration
MSCI and Morningstar Sustainalytics both assess data privacy and security through management of the

risk, exposure to it and the controversies that follow a breach. Weak governance weighs on the score,
and a breach can cut it directly – more severe controversies draw larger deductions that persist for
longer.
Materiality varies sharply by sector. SASB flags data security as material across most consumer,
financial, healthcare and technology industries, with customer privacy concentrated in the most data-
intensive ones (see right).

### Page 20

IV. Investment, risk and opportunity

### Page 21

21
Europe’s dependency on US tech is firmly in the spotlight
Access to frontier AI models is now a security question; the funding gap for Europe will be hard to close
Source: Tikehau Capital – Tikehau Capital Barometer of European Investment in Cybersecurity – 7th edition 2026, Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank Research.
—Growing cyber risk creates increased dependency on US cyber tech. Growing
concern over the cyber capabilities of frontier AI models has made ensuring

access to such technology a national security priority. US dominance over the AI
tech chain (e.g. chips, model development, etc), its willingness to impose AI-
related controls / restrictions, and previous pressure against Europe over tech
regulations has amplified concerns that dependence on US tech represents a
strategic threat to European sovereignty.
—Funding is the structural challenge. US cybersecurity start-ups attract almost 6x
the funding of European peers, and US-domiciled firms dominate the major

indices – 89% of the Nasdaq CTA Cybersecurity index and 93% of Global X
Cybersecurity ETF (which seeks to track the performance of Indxx Cybersecurity
Index) by weight. Europe has also seen home-grown firms such as Darktrace
acquired by US companies, though this reflects broader market trends rather
than anything cyber-specific. Ultimately, the US has a greater exit strategy for
start-ups as well.
—Boosting homegrown solutions a focus, but also a risk. EU policymakers are

seeking to address the dependency; e.g. the EU’s Cybersecurity and AI Action Plan
includes “scaling up Europe’s AI capabilities for cybersecurity” as one of its three
objectives. However, there are also risks associated with this endeavour; as one
dbDataInsights survey participant put it, “for most European organisations, the
bigger near-term danger would be moving too fast to less capable providers.”
—The wider relationship adds to the exposure. A more transactional

international environment, alongside Europe’s dependency on the US for energy
supplies and defence support, compounds the risk. Recent US-EU diesel
negotiations serve as a pertinent example of this, whereby the US reportedly
considered a diesel export ban to compel a release of European diesel reserves.
